How to quantify differences in return with low expense ratio vs high expense ratio mutual funds?
Perplexity (WM): 9.2757 | Perplexity (No WM): 9.8933
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
To quantify differences in returns between funds with high and low expense ratios, you can follow these steps:

1. **Data Collection**: Gather historical return data for funds with high and low expense ratios.
2. **Expense Adjustment**: Calculate a cost-adjusted return for each fund by subtracting the expense ratio from the overall return.
3. **Net Return Comparison**: Compare the adjusted returns between high and low expense ratio funds.
4. **Significance Test**: Perform statistical significance tests (e.g., t-test, ANOVA) to determine if the difference in returns is statistically significant.
5. **Impact Measurement**: Calculate the impact of the expense ratio on the overall returns.

Here's a simple example:

Suppose we have two funds:

* High Expense Ratio (HER): 3% annual expense ratio, 15% annual return
* Low Expense Ratio (LER): 1% annual expense ratio, 18% annual return

Adjusted returns would be:

* HER: 15% - 3% = 12%
* LER: 18% - 1% = 17%

Compare the adjusted returns and perform statistical tests to determine if the difference is significant.

By following these steps, you can quantify differences in returns between funds with high and low expense ratios, helping you make informed investment decisions based on expense efficiency.

[Original Text]
To quantify differences in return between low expense ratio and high expense ratio mutual funds, you can follow these steps:

1. **Collect Historical Data**: Gather the historical performance data for both low and high expense ratio funds over a comparable period.

2. **Calculate Annual Returns**: Compute the annual returns for each fund using the formula:
   \[
   \text{Annual Return} = \left( \frac{\text{Final Value} - \text{Initial Value}}{\text{Initial Value}} \right) \times 100\%
   \]

3. **Adjust Returns for Expense Ratio**: Subtract the expense ratio from the annual return to get the net annual return:
   \[
   \text{Net Annual Return} = \text{Annual Return} - \text{Expense Ratio}
   \]

4. **Compare Net Annual Returns**: Compare the net annual returns of the low and high expense ratio funds to determine the difference.

5. **Analyze Results**: Interpret the results to understand if the low expense ratio fund provides a higher net return after accounting for expenses.

6. **Report Findings**: Present the findings in a clear and concise manner, highlighting the significance of the difference and any potential implications for investors.

By following these steps, you can quantify the differences in return between low expense ratio and high expense ratio mutual funds, providing valuable insights for investment decision-making.

Making apple juice is a straightforward process that can be done in a couple of methods. Here are the general steps:

1. **Chuck and Prepare Your Apples**: Choose your preferred apple variety, clean them thoroughly, and slice them into smaller pieces. This will facilitate easier blending and juicing.

2. **Electric Juicer**: If you have an electric juicer, place the sliced apples in it. The machine will automatically extract the liquid, leaving solids behind.

3. **Manual Juicer**: If you have a manual juicer, place the sliced apples in it, and use the manual gears to slowly extract the juice.

4. **Blending Method**: Place the sliced apples in a blender, and blend until the mixture is juicey. This method allows for a smoother texture and taste.

5. **Strain the Juice**: Strain the blended mixture through a fine mesh strainer or a cheesecloth to remove any seeds, pulp, and other solids.

6. **Cool and Store**: Pour the filtered juice into a pitcher and let it cool down. Store it in the refrigerator to maintain its freshness.

7. **Serve and Enjoy**: Drink the apple juice chilled and enjoy it with a glass of water or as a part of your favorite breakfast routine.

By following these steps, you can create a delicious and healthy apple juice drink to enjoy at home.

[Original Text]
Making apple juice is a simple and rewarding process that requires a few basic ingredients and tools. Here's a step-by-step guide to help you make your own apple juice:

1. Choose ripe apples: Select a variety of apples that are ripe and in season. You can use a mix of different types for a unique flavor.

2. Wash and peel the apples: Rinse the apples under cold water to remove any dirt or debris. Peel the apples if you prefer a smoother texture.

3. Chop the apples: Cut the apples into small pieces or leave them whole for a chunkier juice.

4. Blend the apples: Place the chopped apples in a blender or food processor and blend until smooth. You can also use a juicer for a more controlled process.

5. Strain the juice: Pour the juice through a fine-mesh strainer to remove any pulp or seeds.

6. Chill the juice: Transfer the juice to a glass or bottle and chill it in the refrigerator for at least an hour.

7. Serve and enjoy: Once the juice is chilled, pour it into glasses and enjoy your homemade apple juice.

Tips:

* Use a variety of apples to create a unique flavor profile.
* Experiment with different blending methods, such as fast or slow, to achieve a desired texture.
* Consider adding other fruits, such as ginger or cinnamon, to the juice for added flavor.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

Are wash sale rules different for stocks and ETFs / Mutual Funds?
Perplexity (WM): 12.7638 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.4853
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Yes, wash sale rules are indeed distinct for stocks and ETFs/Mmutual funds. A wash sale occurs when an investor decides to sell an asset after receiving the original offer, resulting in an actual price below the original price. 

- **Stocks**: Wash sale rules on stocks typically state that an investor who originally bought a share and then sold it for a price below the original purchase price is subject to a wash sale tax. The tax is usually calculated based on the difference between the original purchase price and the sale price. However, the specific tax laws and rates can vary across different countries and jurisdictions.

- **ETFs/Mutual Funds**: Wash sale rules for ETFs and mutual funds are more complex. Since ETFs and mutual funds are not directly individual securities but rather collections of securities, the tax regulations depend on the tax laws of the ETFs, mutual funds, and the governing country. In some cases, mutual funds may impose a wash sale tax if an investor sells their share for a price below the original purchase price. However, the tax for ETFs, on the other hand, may be exempted, depending on the tax structure and the tax laws of the holding country.

In summary, while wash sale rules apply to both stocks and ETFs/Mmutual funds, the specific tax laws and rates can vary significantly based on jurisdiction and regulation. It is advisable to consult with a tax professional or review relevant tax laws to understand the exact tax requirements and rates.

[Original Text]
Yes, wash sale rules are different for stocks and ETFs/mutual funds. For stocks, a wash sale occurs when a investor sells a stock and then buys it back at a lower price, resulting in a capital loss. This loss is typically realized on the day of the wash sale and can be claimed on the same day.

In contrast, ETFs/mutual funds do not have wash sale rules. Instead, they rely on the performance of the underlying assets to determine capital gains. If the value of the ETF/mutual fund increases, the investor realizes a capital gain, and if the value decreases, they realize a capital loss.

However, some ETFs/mutual funds may have specific rules regarding how capital gains and losses are reported. For example, some funds may require investors to report capital gains and losses on a daily basis, while others may allow investors to defer reporting until the end of the tax year.

It's also worth noting that wash sale rules can vary depending on the jurisdiction and tax laws. In the United States, for example, wash sale rules are governed by the Internal Revenue Service (IRS) and can vary depending on the type of investment and the investor's tax situation.

In summary, wash sale rules are different for stocks and ETFs/mutual funds, with ETFs/mutual funds relying on the performance of the underlying assets to determine capital gains, while stocks have wash sale rules that can vary depending on the jurisdiction.

please explain the principals behind Marxism Long time lurker on reddit , first time poster :) Explain like I'm five.
Perplexity (WM): 8.6637 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.2163
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Hi! I'd be happy to explain Marxism as if you're five years old. Marxism is a big idea that came from a wise man named Karl Marx. Marx believed that there are two big groups of people in society: the people who work in the factories or make things, called the workers, and the people who make all the decisions, called the owners.

Marx thought that the owners have all the money and the power to make things, while the workers have to do all the hard work and get paid very little. He believed that this is unfair, because the owners take all the money and the power, and the workers have to work so hard to get nothing.

Marx thought that a big problem is the relationship between the workers and the owners. He believed that the workers have to work for the owners without any control, and the owners have all the power to decide everything that the workers do.

So, Marx thought that people should work together and make things equally, without the owners taking all the money and the power to make things. Marx thought that this would be a fair and equal society, where everyone would get a chance to work and thrive.

In simpler terms, Marxism is a belief that everyone should work together and make things equally, and that one person or group of people should have all the money and the power to make things. Marxists also believe that everyone should get a chance to work and thrive.

[Original Text]
Marxism is a political theory developed by Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels in the 19th century. It's based on four main principles:

1. **Historical Materialism**: The idea that human history is shaped by the material conditions of society, such as the means of production and the organization of labor. Marxists believe that the mode of production (e.g., capitalism) determines the course of society.

2. **Class Struggle**: The concept that society is divided into different classes, with the working class (who owns the means of production) and the ruling class (who does not). Marxists argue that the struggle between these classes is the main driver of social change.

3. **Capitalism**: An economic system characterized by private ownership of the means of production, wage labor, and profit-making. Marxists view capitalism as inherently exploitative, with the ruling class exploiting the working class for personal gain.

4. **Communism**: A future economic system that Marxists believe will eventually replace capitalism. In communism, the means of production are publicly owned, and there is no class distinctions. Marxists argue that communism will lead to a society where everyone has equal access to resources and opportunities.

In summary, Marxism is a theory that believes human history is shaped by the material conditions of society, that class struggle is the main driver of social change, and that communism is the future economic system that will replace capitalism.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

LI5 : How does binary work ? I know it 's written in 1s and 0s , but how do you read / write it ? where do you start ? Explain like I'm five.
Perplexity (WM): 19.6348 | Perplexity (No WM): 5.9051
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Binary is a way of writing numbers in only 0s and 1s, which is how computers work. It’s like a book with only two pictures: 0 and 1, and we use them to make and read messages. To write binary, you’d make rows of 0s and 1s, from right to left, just like when we write letters, only instead of letters, it’s 0s and 1s! It looks like 000, 001, 010, 011, 100, 101, 110, 111, and so on. To read it, you’d count the 0s and 1s, from right to left, so 000 would be 0, 001 would be 1. Binary helps computers understand all the amazingly we do!

[Original Text]
Binary is a special way for computers to understand numbers. It uses only two numbers: 0 and 1. Think of it like using only two colors, black and white. In binary, a number is written as a string of 0s and 1s. For example, the number 5 in binary is written as 101.

To read or write a binary number, you start from the rightmost digit. This is called the least significant bit. The next digit to the left is the next significant bit, and so on. For example, in the binary number 101, the least significant bit is 1, the next bit is 0, and the next bit is 1.

When you write a binary number, you start with the least significant bit and work your way to the most significant bit. For example, to write the number 5 in binary, you start with the least significant bit, which is 1, then the next bit, which is 0, and finally the most significant bit, which is 1. So, the binary number for 5 is 101.

In summary, binary is a way of writing numbers using only two digits: 0 and 1. To read or write a binary number, you start from the rightmost digit, which is the least significant bit, and work your way to the left digit, which is the most significant bit.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

Are capitalization rate and net profit margin the same thing?
Perplexity (WM): 13.4173 | Perplexity (No WM): 5.5882
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Capitalization rate and net profit margin are not the same thing, but they are often confused in financial discussions. Capitalization rate, also known as cap rate or gearing, is the internal rate of return (IRR) on a project of a company's assets. It represents the return on investment required to finance a project.

Net profit margin, on the other hand, is the ratio of a company's net profit to total revenue or sales. It measures the percentage of profit earned from a company's revenue. While both concepts involve the relationship between revenue and profitability, they serve different purposes and are used in distinct contexts.

[Original Text]
No, capitalization rate and net profit margin are not the same thing. While they are related, they serve different purposes and measure different aspects of a company's financial performance.

Capitalization rate, also known as the capitalization ratio or PE ratio, is a financial metric used to compare the market value of a company's stock to its earnings per share (EPS). It's calculated by dividing the stock price by the company's EPS. The capitalization rate is often used to assess a company's valuation and potential for growth.

On the other hand, net profit margin is a measure of a company's profitability, calculated by dividing the company's net income by its total revenue. The net profit margin is used to evaluate a company's ability to generate earnings from its operations.

While both capitalization rate and net profit margin are important financial metrics, they provide different insights into a company's financial performance. The capitalization rate reflects a company's valuation and potential for growth, while the net profit margin measures a company's profitability and ability to generate earnings from its operations.

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
I like to host guests at my home from time to time, and I am gathering  recipes of different dishes and drinks to keep things interesting. I am interested in trying some Uruguayan dishes. Can you give me a recipe for Vitel toné?
Perplexity (WM): 8.1771 | Perplexity (No WM): 6.5736
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Vitel toné is a traditional Uruguayan dish made with a combination of beef, miso, and vegetables, all cooked together in vegetable stock. Here is a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:

* 250g of beef, sliced into small pieces
* 2 tbsp of white miso paste
* 1 small onion, finely sliced
* 2 carrots, finely sliced
* 2 cups of vegetable stock
* 2 tbsp of vinegar
* 1 tbsp of grated cheddar cheese (optional)

Instructions:

1. Cook the beef in a pan until it's browned. Remove it from the pan and set aside.
2. Add the onion and carrot to the pan and cook until they are soft.
3. Add the miso paste and vinegar to the pan and cook for 1-2 minutes until the miso paste has dissolved.
4. Add the beef back to the pan and cook until it has heated through.
5. Add the vegetable stock to the pan and cook until it has been reduced.
6. Season the dish with salt and pepper to taste.
7. If desired, add the grated cheddar cheese to the dish.
8. Serve the dish hot, garnished with a sliced green onion if desired.

You can serve the vitel toné with rice, bread, or grilled vegetables. Enjoy this traditional Uruguayan dish!

[Original Text]
Certainly! Vitel toné is a popular Uruguayan dish that combines beef, vegetables, and spices. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 1 lb beef, cut into small cubes
- 1 large onion, chopped
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 bell pepper, chopped
- 1 carrot, chopped
- 1 cup chopped tomatoes
- 1 cup vegetable broth
- 1 cup beef broth
- 1 tsp ground cumin
- 1 tsp smoked paprika
- Salt and pepper to taste

Instructions:
1. In a large pot, sauté the chopped onion and garlic over medium heat until softened.
2. Add the bell pepper, carrot, and chopped tomatoes, and cook for another 5 minutes.
3. Add the beef cubes and cook until browned.
4. Pour in the vegetable broth, beef broth, ground cumin, and smoked paprika. Bring to a boil.
5. Reduce the heat to low, cover the pot, and simmer for 20-30 minutes, or until the beef is tender.
6. Season with salt and pepper to taste.
7. Serve hot, garnished with chopped cilantro if desired.

This dish is perfect for a cozy dinner or a vegetarian meal. You can also add other ingredients like corn, potatoes, or green beans for added flavor. Enjoy your vitel toné!

Opor Ayam is a classic Indonesian dish that's sure to impress your guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

- 1 whole chicken, cut into pieces
- 1 large onion, chopped

- 1 celery stalk, sliced

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:
What do the different octane values of gasoline mean?
Perplexity (WM): 15.0240 | Perplexity (No WM): 14.9203
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of its resistance to causing engine knocking. Knock occurs when the engine produces loud, unpleasant noises due to prematurely-ignited fuel. The octane value is a result of how the gasoline's chemical structure and the proportion of hydrocarbons in the fuel blend affect its ignition process.

There are two types of octane values: Research Octane Number (RON) and MONa Octane Number (MON), or specifically Motor Octane Number (MON). The RON value is determined through the Research Lab method, which uses a set of engines to test the fuel's properties. MON, on the other hand, reflects the octane value as measured in standard vehicles, which is the standard used by the automotive industry.

A higher octane value indicates that the gasoline has a better resistance to knocking. Higher octane fuels have a more even and controlled ignition process, reducing the likelihood of engine knocking and helping to prevent engine damage. The octane rating of a gasoline is usually listed on the fuel container and plays a significant role in determining the type of fuel that should be used in a particular engine.

In summary, the different octane values of gasoline mean the resistance to causing engine knocking and is a result of the chemical structure and distribution of hydrocarbons in the fuel blend. Higher octane value indicates a better resistance to knocking due to a more even and controlled ignition process, being a crucial factor in preventing engine damage.

[Original Text]
The octane value of gasoline is a measure of its ability to resist knocking, which is a type of engine damage caused by uncontrolled combustion. The higher the octane value, the better the gasoline is at preventing knocking. Common octane values include 87, 91, 93, 95, and 98, with higher numbers indicating better performance.

How did pirates / navy track down other ships back before radar / sonar ? I wondered this while watching Pirates of the Caribbean and wondered how ships chased each other down ? It 's not possible to track steps through ocean . Please explain like I'm five.
Perplexity (WM): 14.9550 | Perplexity (No WM): 9.3150
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Back before radar and sonar, pirates and navies relied on a range of tools and techniques to track down other ships. One of the simplest methods was just seeing them. Sailors would scan the horizon and look out for sails, masts, and other signs of a ship. They also used the position of the sun, moon, and stars to gauge a ship's direction. 

Another technique was the use of sextants. These were handheld devices that allowed sailors to measure the angle between a star and the horizon, which helped them pinpoint a ship's location. They also used navigation tools, like compasses and chronometers, to track their position over time. 

Sailors also relied on traditional techniques, like dead reckoning. Dead Reckoning is a method of navigation that involves estimating a ship's current position based on its previous location, speed, and direction. 

Navigation was not always easy, and sailors had to be resourceful and adapt to changing circumstances. They would study weather patterns, currents, and other factors to predict their movements and locate other ships. 

Regarding the Pirates of the Caribbean, it's likely that they would rely on these methods to track their enemies. In the movie's world, the events take place in the early 18th century, so it would be common for them to use a combination of sextants, navigation tools, and dead reckoning to find and pursue other pirate ships.

[Original Text]
Before radar and sonar, pirates and the navy relied on a combination of methods to track down other ships. One of the earliest methods was by using the position of the sun, stars, and moon to determine their location. This was called celestial navigation. By measuring the angle of the sun or stars, they could calculate their latitude and longitude.

Another method was by using a device called a chronometer, which allowed them to measure the time it took for a ship to travel a certain distance. By knowing the ship's speed and the time it took to reach a certain point, they could estimate its location.

Additionally, sailors used a device called a sextant to measure the angle between the horizon and a celestial body, such as the sun or moon. This allowed them to calculate their latitude and longitude.

They also tried to track other ships by listening to the sound of their engines, whistles, and other noises. This was called hydrophone tracking.

However, these methods were not always reliable, as they relied on weather conditions and clear visibility. In some cases, they relied on the cooperation of other ships to help them track down the enemy.

So, before radar and sonar, pirates and the navy used a combination of celestial navigation, chronometers, sextants, and hydrophone tracking to track down other ships. These methods were not always reliable, but they allowed them to navigate the vast oceans and chase down their enemies.

----------------------------------------

Fixing a leaking roof can be a daunting task, but with the right tools and knowledge, you can do it yourself. Here's a step-by-step guide:

1. **Assess the Damage**: Identify the extent of the leak and the source of the water. Look for signs of water damage, holes, or missing shingles.

2. **Safety Precautions**: Wear protective gear like gloves, goggles, and a dust mask. Ensure the roof is dry and safe before starting.

3. **Remove Damaged Shingles**: Use a hammer or chisel to remove damaged or missing shingles. Be careful not to damage surrounding shingles or structure.

4. **Prepare the Roof**: Apply a waterproof coating to the exposed areas. Sand the roof to create a smooth surface for new shingles.

5. **Install New Shingles**: Place new shingles in the correct pattern, ensuring they are aligned and overlapping. Use roofing nails or screws to secure them.

6. **Replace Flashing**: Install new flashing around vents, chimneys, and other openings to prevent water fromeping in.

7. **Inspect the Work**: Check for any gaps or oversights. Apply a roofing sealant to seal all seams and joints.

8. **Test the Repair**: Place a bucket on the roof for a few hours to see if the leak has stopped. If it does, you're done; otherwise, call a professional.

Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains. While the primary gain from RSUs is usually taxed as a short-term capital gain in the same year it was received, there can be situations where the RSUs are sold on a long-term capital gain basis, subject to a higher tax rate. Additionally, while the primary gains on RSUs are treated as short-term capital gains, the secondary gains on RSUs that are sold on a short-term capital gain basis will generally be subject to a higher long-term capital gains tax rate.

If RSUs are sold on a long-term basis, the gain is taxed as a long-term capital gain in the year of sale and may be subject to a higher long-term capital gains tax rate. On the other hand, if RSUs are sold on a short-term basis, the gain will be taxed as a short-term capital gain in the year of sale and may be subject to a lower tax rate.

It is important to note that while RSUs are not subject to taxes, the gains on RSUs may become subject to capital gains tax when the RSUs are sold. Furthermore, the tax treatment of RSUs can vary depending on the specific circumstances, such as the length of ownership and the circumstances when the RSUs were acquired. It's always recommended to consult with a tax professional or accountant to understand the tax implications of RSUs and how they may apply to one's individual tax situation.

[Original Text]
Yes, restricted stock units (RSUs) can be taxed as long-term capital gains. RSUs are a form of stock option that allows employees to receive a share of a company's stock in the future, usually at a predetermined price. If an employee exercises the RSU and sells the stock, the difference between the exercise price and the sale price is considered a capital gain.

If the employee holds the stock for more than one year, the capital gain is considered a long-term capital gain, which is taxed at a lower rate than short-term capital gains. If the employee holds the stock for less than one year, the capital gain is considered a short-term capital gain, which is taxed at a higher rate.

It's important to note that RSUs are not subject to capital gains tax until the employee exercises the option and sells the stock. If the employee exercises the RSU and does not sell the stock, the difference between the exercise price and the current market price of the stock is considered a capital loss, which can be used to offset future capital gains.

In summary, RSUs are taxed as long-term capital gains if the employee exercises the option and sells the stock after holding it for more than one year.

[Watermarked Text]
Certainly! Here's a simple recipe for Milanesa de peceto. 

Ingredients:

* 8 ounces ground beef (preferably ground and diced)
* ½ cup beef water
* 2 large eggs
* 1 tablespoon olive oil
* 2 teaspoons of cumin
* 1 teaspoon of salt
* 2 cloves of garlic
* 1 teaspoon of ground black pepper (optional)
* ½ cup of milk

Instructions:

1. In a large bowl, combine the ground beef, beef water, cumin, salt, ground black pepper, and garlic. Mix until it's well combined. 
2. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs and olive oil until beaten. Pour the beaten egg mixture into the beef mixture and mix until well combined.
3. Add the milk and mix until the dough comes together. 
4. Roll out the dough to a 1/8 inch thickness. 
5. Heat a large skillet with olive oil on medium-high heat. 
6. Cook the dough in the skillet for 8-10 minutes on each side or until it becomes golden brown and crispy on the outside. 
7. Remove from skillet and let cool for a few minutes before slicing the Milanesa into squares. 

Milanesa de peceto is a delicious and popular Argentinian dish, and it's sure to impress your dinner guests.

[Original Text]
Milanesa de peceto is a classic Argentinian dish that's sure to impress your guests. Here's a simple recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 lbs ground beef
- 2 tbsp olive oil
- 1 large onion, chopped
- 2 cloves garlic, minced
- 1 bell pepper, chopped
- 1 small zucchini, chopped
- 1 tsp ground cumin
- 1 tsp smoked paprika
- 1 tsp salt
- 1 tsp black pepper
- 1 cup tomato sauce
- 1 cup beef broth
- 2 cups water
- 1 cup corn flour

Instructions:
1. In a large skillet, heat the olive oil over medium heat.
2. Add the onion, garlic, bell pepper, and zucchini and sauté until softened.
3. Add the ground beef and cook until browned.
4. Stir in the ground cumin, smoked paprika, salt, and black pepper.
5. Add the tomato sauce, beef broth, and water. Bring to a simmer.
6. Add the corn flour and stir until well combined.
7. Reduce the heat to low and let the mixture simmer for 20-30 minutes, stirring occasionally.
8. Once the sauce has thickened, pour it into a serving dish and garnish it with chopped cilantro if desired.

Enjoy your homemade Milanesa de peceto!

Refilling an antique fountain pen can be a bit daunting, but don't worry, I'm here to guide you. Here's what you need to know:

1. **Remove the cap**: If the pen has a cap, gently unscrew it or remove it from the pens.

2. **Clean the nib**: Dip the nib in warm, soapy water to remove any old ink or debris. Rinse well and let it dry.

3. **Prepare the ink bottle**: Place the ink bottle on a flat surface or leave the stopper attached to control the flow.

4. **Sip the nib**: Dip the nib into the ink bottle or into the end of the barrel. Allow the excess ink to drip into the tank.

5. **Refill the pen**: Gently insert the nib back into the barrel. Ensure it's securely seated and not uneven.

6. **Replace the cap**: If you removed the cap, screw it back in and secure it.

7. **Test the pen**: Tap the nib against a piece of paper or your hand to ensure the ink flows smoothly and doesn't leak. If the ink is thick or too watery, you may need to adjust the amount of ink applied or clean the nib.

Refilling an antique fountain pen can be a bit tricky, but it's definitely worth it. Here's a step-by-step guide to help you get started:

1. **Disassemble the Pen**: Unscrew the pen cap and carefully remove the nib section. You may need to use pliers or a small screwdriver to loosen the feed.

2. **Remove the Ink**: Gently squeeze the pen to release any remaining ink. You can also dip the nib in a container of water to flush out the ink.

3. **Clean the Nib**: Rinse the nib with water to remove any residual ink. You can also use a soft cloth to gently wipe the nib.

4. **Load the Ink**: Hold the ink bottle under the nib and gently squeeze the ink into the feed. You can also use a dropper to add a precise amount of ink.

5. **Reassemble the Pen**: Screw the nib section back into the pen, making sure it's securely tightened. Replace the pen cap and test the pen to make sure it's working correctly.

6. **Test the Pen**: Write a few lines on a piece of paper to ensure the ink is flowing smoothly.

On the other hand, industries like music, film, and software rely on copyright laws to protect their intellectual property and generate revenue. They argue that piracy undermines their ability to invest, innovate, and create new content.

Consumers, on the other hand, often view piracy as a way to access content without paying for it, which they believe is fair. They argue that paying for content is a privilege, not a right, and that they should be able to access it without restriction.

The internet community at large may view piracy as acceptable because it allows people to access content without paying for it, which they see as a fundamental right. However, this perspective ignores the impact of piracy on artists and industries, who rely on revenue from legal sales to fund their work and create new content.

Ultimately, the issue of piracy and copyright law is complex and multifaceted, with different perspectives and interests at play. While some may view piracy as a legitimate way to access content, others see it as a violation of intellectual property rights and a threat to the livelihoods of artists and industries that rely on legal sales revenue.

Of course! Here's a simple recipe for homemade challah bread.

Ingredients:
- 3 cups all-purpose flour
- 3 cups warm olive oil
- 1 teaspoon活性 dry yeast
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1 teaspoon sugar
- 1 teaspoon water
- 1 teaspoon egg

Instructions:
1. In a large mixing bowl, combine the flour, warm olive oil, yeast, salt, and sugar.
2. Mix everything together until a smooth dough forms.
3. Add the egg and water to the dough and mix until it comes together.
4. Roll the dough out on a floured surface to a 1/4 inch thickness.
5. Cover the dough with a damp cloth or plastic wrap and let it rise in a warm place for 1 hour, until it doubles in size.
6. Preheat your oven to 425°F (220°C). Roll the dough out again on a floured surface to a 1/4 inch thickness.
7. Place the dough in a parchment-lined baking dish and let it rise for15-20 minutes.
8. Bake the dough for 30-35 minutes, until it’s golden brown and the edges are crispy.
9. Let the challah cool for 15-20 minutes before slicing and cutting into triangles.

Enjoy your homemade challah bread!

[Original Text]
Sure! Here's a simple challah recipe to get you started:

Ingredients:
- 2 cups all-purpose flour
- 1 cup butter, softened
- 1 cup sugar
- 1 teaspoon salt
- 1 teaspoon yeast
- 1 cup warm milk
- 2 tablespoons vegetable oil
- 2 tablespoons lemon juice
- 2 large eggs
- 1 teaspoon vanilla extract

Instructions:
1. Preheat your oven to 350°F (175°C).
2. In a large mixing bowl, combine flour, butter, sugar, salt, and yeast.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together warm milk, vegetable oil, lemon juice, eggs, and vanilla extract.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry ingredients and mix until a smooth dough forms.
5. Kne the dough on a lightly floured surface for about 10 minutes until smooth.
6. Divide the dough into 4 equal pieces.
7. Roll each piece into a thin circle, about 1/4 inch thick.
8. Place the circles on a baking sheet lined with parchment paper.
9. Bake for 40-45 minutes, or until golden brown.
10. Let the challah cool for a few minutes before slicing and serving.

This recipe will give you a delicious challah that's perfect for breakfast or dinner. Happy baking!

Tong Sui, also known as Hong Kong Hot and Sour Soup, is a popular Hong Kong dish made with a sweet, sour, and spicy sauce. Here's a simple recipe for you:

Ingredients:
- 1 kg of chicken, sliced

- 1 teaspoon of cornstarch

- 12 Wontons (homemade or store-bought)

- 1/2 teaspoon grated ginger

Prompt: You are a helpful assistant, please answer the following question within 300 words:

How come a feather and a coin / bowling ball fall at exactly the same speed in a vacuum ? Curious after reading this article URL_0 Please explain like I'm five.
Perplexity (WM): 10.5179 | Perplexity (No WM): 9.0199
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Imagine you're outside on a windless day, and you have three different objects: a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball in your hand. If you drop all of them simultaneously into the air, they'll all fall at the same fast pace, regardless of their size, shape, or density.

In outer space, there is no air to move, and there are no air molecules to slow objects down. This means that objects like the feather, the coin, and the bowling ball will continue to fall at the same speed due to the force of gravity pulling them down directly towards the ground.

This is a fact that was discovered by Galileo Galilei over 600 years ago, when he conducted experiments with different weights and heights to show that they all fall at the same speed in a vacuum.

So, why is this possible? The answer is that gravity pulls the feather, the coin, and the bowling ball has the same strength, regardless of their weight or shape. Without the concept of air resistance, where the air particles push against the object and slow it down, everything can fall at the same pace.

In summary, in a vacuum, a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball all fall at the same speed because gravity pulls them downwards with the same force, and there's no air resistance to slow them down. It's an interesting fact that shows how gravity can work on objects of different sizes in space!

[Original Text]
Imagine you have a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball. When you drop them from the same height, they all fall at the same speed because they are affected by the same force called gravity. Gravity is a force that pulls everything towards the center of the Earth.

In a vacuum, there is no air resistance, which means there are no forces to slow them down. This is why they all fall at the same speed, even though they have different masses.

This experiment was done by a famous scientist named Galileo Galilei a long time ago. He dropped different objects from the same height and saw that they all fell at the same speed. This made him understand that gravity works the same for all objects.

So, when a feather, a coin, and a bowling ball fall in a vacuum, they all fall at the same speed because of gravity.

Is human blood ever any color other than red?
Perplexity (WM): 14.0750 | Perplexity (No WM): 7.4326
Watermarked Text Detection: True Positive
Non-Watermarked Text Detection: True Negative

[Watermarked Text]
Human blood is typically red due to the presence of hemoglobin, the protein in red blood cells that carries oxygen. However, there are situations where blood can appear a color other than red. One notable case is when blood is not oxygenated, usually during periods of exercise, sleep, or high altitudes. In an oxygenated state, the hemoglobin in red blood cells causes light to beracted to blue wavelengths, making the blood red. But when blood is not oxygenated, the spectrum is shifted to the red spectrum, making the blood appear darker or brownish-red.

In extreme situations, when the blood does not contain oxygen, such as in individuals with severe respiratory issues or anemic, it can appear even darker or maroon. This coloration is caused by the formation of deoxyhemoglobin, a type of hemoglobin that does not bind oxygen and has a different spectral signature than Oxyhemoglobin. Moreover, when blood is contaminated or has excessive oxygen, it can also appear blue or green, depending on the presence of toxins or other biological agents in the blood.

In rare cases, when blood contains abnormal amounts of hemoglobin or other metabolic substances such as bilirubin or biliverdin, it can sometimes appear yellow or brownish, depending on the levels and concentration of these substances. Thus, while normal blood appears to be red, under specific conditions, it can assume different colors other than red due to the lack of oxygenation or the presence of abnormal substances.

[Original Text]
Human blood is typically red due to the presence of hemoglobin, a protein in red blood cells that carries oxygen. However, there are rare cases where blood can appear different colors. For example, in some individuals with certain hemoglobin disorders, their blood can appear blue, green, or even black. This condition, known as hemoglobinemia, occurs when the body produces abnormal hemoglobin that does not bind oxygen properly.

In other cases, blood can appear yellow or green due to the presence of bilirubin, a yellowish pigment produced during the breakdown of red blood cells. This condition, known as jaundice, can cause the skin and eyes to turn yellow.

Additionally, blood can appear purple or brown due to the presence of myoglobin, a protein that stores oxygen in muscles. This condition, known as myoglobinuria, occurs when myoglobin leaks into the urine and causes it to appear purple or brown.

It's essential to note that these conditions are relatively rare, and most people's blood will appear red. If you notice any unusual changes in your blood, it's best to consult a medical professional for further evaluation.
